    13→// TestOpenCodeHookFlow verifies the full hook flow for OpenCode:
    14→// session-start → turn-start → file changes → turn-end → checkpoint → commit → condense → session-end.
    15→func TestOpenCodeHookFlow(t *testing.T) {
    16→	t.Parallel()
    17→
    18→	RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
    19→		env.InitEntireWithAgent(strategyName, agent.AgentNameOpenCode)
    20→
    21→		// Create OpenCode session
    22→		session := env.NewOpenCodeSession()
    23→
    24→		// 1. session-start
    25→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
    26→			t.Fatalf("session-start error: %v", err)
    27→		}
    28→
    29→		// 2. turn-start (equivalent to UserPromptSubmit — captures pre-prompt state)
    30→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath, "Add a feature"); err != nil {
    31→			t.Fatalf("turn-start error: %v", err)
    32→		}
    33→
    34→		// 3. Agent makes file changes (AFTER turn-start so they're detected as new)
    35→		env.WriteFile("feature.go", "package main\n// new feature")
    36→
    37→		// 4. Create transcript with the file change
    38→		session.CreateOpenCodeTranscript("Add a feature", []FileChange{
    39→			{Path: "feature.go", Content: "package main\n// new feature"},
    40→		})
    41→
    42→		// 5. turn-end (equivalent to Stop — creates checkpoint)
    43→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
    44→			t.Fatalf("turn-end error: %v", err)
    45→		}
    46→
    47→		// 6. Verify checkpoint was created
    48→		points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    49→		if len(points) == 0 {
    50→			t.Fatal("expected at least 1 rewind point after turn-end")
    51→		}
    52→
    53→		// 7. For manual-commit, user commits manually (triggers condensation).
    54→		// For auto-commit, the commit was already made during turn-end.
    55→		if strategyName == strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit {
    56→			env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add feature", "feature.go")
    57→		}
    58→
    59→		// 8. session-end
    60→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeSessionEnd(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
    61→			t.Fatalf("session-end error: %v", err)
    62→		}
    63→
    64→		// 9. Verify condensation happened (checkpoint on metadata branch)
    65→		checkpointID := env.TryGetLatestCheckpointID()
    66→		if checkpointID == "" {
    67→			t.Fatal("expected checkpoint on metadata branch after commit")
    68→		}
    69→
    70→		// 10. Verify condensed data
    71→		transcriptPath := SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.TranscriptFileName)
    72→		_, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, transcriptPath)
    73→		if !found {
    74→			t.Error("condensed transcript should exist on metadata branch")
    75→		}
    76→	})
    77→}

    79→// TestOpenCodeAgentStrategyComposition verifies that the OpenCode agent and strategy

    81→func TestOpenCodeAgentStrategyComposition(t *testing.T) {
    82→	t.Parallel()
    83→
    84→	RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {

    96→
    97→		// Create session and transcript for agent interface testing.

   103→		})
   104→
   105→		// Read session via agent interface

   110→		if err != nil {

   114→		// Verify agent computed modified files

   118→
   119→		// Simulate session flow: session-start → turn-start → file changes → turn-end
   120→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {
   121→			t.Fatalf("session-start error = %v", err)
   122→		}
   123→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, "Add a feature"); err != nil {

   125→		}
   126→
   127→		// Create the actual file AFTER turn-start so the strategy detects it as new
   128→		env.WriteFile("feature.go", "package main\n// new feature")
   129→
   130→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {

   132→		}

   138→		}
   139→	})
   140→}

   142→// TestOpenCodeRewind verifies that rewind works with OpenCode checkpoints.

   155→		t.Fatalf("session-start error: %v", err)
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	// Turn 1: create file1.go (AFTER turn-start so it's detected as new)

   163→	env.WriteFile("file1.go", "package main\n// file1 v1")

   167→

   172→	points1 := env.GetRewindPoints()

   176→	checkpoint1ID := points1[0].ID
   177→
   178→	// Turn 2: modify file1 and create file2 (AFTER turn-start)
   179→	if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, "Modify file1"); err != nil {
   180→		t.Fatalf("turn-start error: %v", err)

   183→	env.WriteFile("file1.go", "package main\n// file1 v2")

   187→		{Path: "file2.go", Content: "package main\n// file2"},

   189→
   190→	if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {

   193→
   194→	// Verify 2 checkpoints
   195→	points2 := env.GetRewindPoints()

   199→
   200→	// Rewind to first checkpoint

   205→	// Verify file1 is restored to v1

> AGENT

No. None of the existing tests cover this scenario. Here's what the existing OpenCode integration tests test:

1. **`TestOpenCodeHookFlow`** - Single turn: new file → checkpoint → commit → condense. No resumed session.

2. **`TestOpenCodeRewind`** - Two turns in the SAME session, but **no commit between turns**. The file stays untracked for both turns, so `changes.New` catches it both times.

3. **`TestOpenCodeMultiTurnCondensation`** - Single turn → commit → session-end. No second turn.

The missing scenario is:
1. Turn 1: create new file → checkpoint → **commit** (condense, shadow branch deleted)
2. Turn 2 (resumed): **modify the now-tracked file** → checkpoint should be created

The key is that after commit, the file becomes tracked. In turn 2, it's **modified** (not new), so `changes.New` won't find it. It must be found via transcript extraction — which was broken because `extractFilePathFromInput` didn't recognize OpenCode's `"filePath"` key.

Also worth noting: **the existing test data uses `"file_path"` (snake_case)** while real OpenCode exports use `"filePath"` (camelCase), so the existing tests were passing with the wrong key format and didn't catch this at the unit test level either.

Would you like me to add an integration test for this resumed-session-after-commit scenario?

> DEVELOPER

yes, and fix the test data

   217→// TestOpenCodeMultiTurnCondensation verifies that multiple turns in a session
   218→// are correctly condensed when the user commits.
   219→func TestOpenCodeMultiTurnCondensation(t *testing.T) {
   220→	t.Parallel()
   221→
   222→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
   223→	env.InitEntireWithAgent(strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit, agent.AgentNameOpenCode)
   224→

   226→	transcriptPath := session.TranscriptPath
   227→
   228→	// session-start
   229→	if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {
   230→		t.Fatalf("session-start error: %v", err)
   231→	}
   232→
   233→	// Turn 1: create file (AFTER turn-start so it's detected as new)
   234→	if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, "Create app.go"); err != nil {
   235→		t.Fatalf("turn-start error: %v", err)
   236→	}

   245→	}
   246→
   247→	// Verify checkpoint

   249→	if len(points) == 0 {
   250→		t.Fatal("expected rewind point after first turn")
   251→	}

   253→	// Commit with hooks (triggers condensation)

   255→
   256→	// session-end

   259→	}
   260→

   930→			"state": map[string]interface{}{

   933→				"output": "File written: " + change.Path,
   934→			},
   935→		})
   936→	}
   937→	parts = append(parts, map[string]interface{}{

   939→		"text": "Done!",
   940→	})
   941→
   942→	messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   943→		"info": map[string]interface{}{
   944→			"id":   fmt.Sprintf("msg-%d", s.msgCounter),
   945→			"role": "assistant",
   946→			"time": map[string]interface{}{

   948→				"completed": 1708300000 + s.msgCounter + 5,
   949→			},
   950→			"tokens": map[string]interface{}{
   951→				"input":     150,
   952→				"output":    80,
   953→				"reasoning": 10,
   954→				"cache":     map[string]int{"read": 5, "write": 15},
   955→			},
   956→			"cost": 0.003,
   957→		},
   958→		"parts": parts,
   959→	})
   960→
   961→	// Build export session format
   962→	exportSession := map[string]interface{}{
   963→		"info": map[string]interface{}{
   964→			"id": s.ID,
   965→		},
   966→		"messages": messages,
   967→	}
   968→
   969→	// Ensure directory exists
   970→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(s.TranscriptPath), 0o755); err != nil {
   971→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to create transcript dir: %v", err)
   972→	}
   973→
   974→	// Write export JSON transcript
   975→	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(exportSession, "", "  ")
   976→	if err != nil {
   977→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal transcript: %v", err)
   978→	}
   979→	if err := os.WriteFile(s.TranscriptPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   980→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   981→	}
   982→
   983→	return s.TranscriptPath
   984→}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/opencode_hooks_test.go",
  "old_string": "\tenv.ValidateCheckpoint(CheckpointValidation{\n\t\tCheckpointID: checkpointID,\n\t\tStrategy:     strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit,\n\t\tFilesTouched: []string{\"app.go\"},\n\t\tExpectedTranscriptContent: []string{\n\t\t\t\"Create app.go\", // User prompt should appear in transcript\n\t\t},\n\t})\n}",
  "new_string": "\tenv.ValidateCheckpoint(CheckpointValidation{\n\t\tCheckpointID: checkpointID,\n\t\tStrategy:     strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit,\n\t\tFilesTouched: []string{\"app.go\"},\n\t\tExpectedTranscriptContent: []string{\n\t\t\t\"Create app.go\", // User prompt should appear in transcript\n\t\t},\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestOpenCodeResumedSessionAfterCommit verifies that resuming an OpenCode session\n// after a commit correctly creates a checkpoint for the second turn.\n//\n// This tests the scenario:\n//  1. Turn 1: create new file → checkpoint → user commits (condensation)\n//  2. Turn 2 (resumed): modify the now-tracked file → checkpoint should be created\n//\n// Bug: transcript-based file extraction used \"file_path\" key but OpenCode exports use\n// \"filePath\" (camelCase). Turn 1 worked because the file was NEW (detected via git status).\n// Turn 2 failed because the file was now tracked+modified — only detectable via transcript\n// extraction, which silently returned empty due to the key mismatch.\nfunc TestOpenCodeResumedSessionAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\n\tRunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\tenv.InitEntireWithAgent(strategyName, agent.AgentNameOpenCode)\n\n\t\tsession := env.NewOpenCodeSession()\n\t\ttranscriptPath := session.TranscriptPath\n\n\t\t// === Turn 1: Create a new file ===\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"session-start error: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, \"Create app.go\"); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"turn-start 1 error: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tenv.WriteFile(\"app.go\", \"package main\\nfunc main() {}\")\n\t\tsession.CreateOpenCodeTranscript(\"Create app.go\", []FileChange{\n\t\t\t{Path: \"app.go\", Content: \"package main\\nfunc main() {}\"},\n\t\t})\n\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"turn-end 1 error: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tpoints1 := env.GetRewindPoints()\n\t\tif len(points1) == 0 {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected rewind point after turn 1\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// === User commits (triggers condensation, deletes shadow branch) ===\n\t\tenv.GitCommitWithShadowHooks(\"Create app\", \"app.go\")\n\n\t\t// Verify condensation happened\n\t\tcheckpointID := env.TryGetLatestCheckpointID()\n\t\tif checkpointID == \"\" {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected checkpoint on metadata branch after commit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// === Turn 2 (resumed): Modify the now-tracked file ===\n\t\t// The file is already committed — it will show as \"modified\" in git status,\n\t\t// NOT as \"new\". This means it must be detected via transcript extraction.\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, \"Add color output\"); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"turn-start 2 error: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tenv.WriteFile(\"app.go\", \"package main\\nimport \\\"fmt\\\"\\nfunc main() { fmt.Println(\\\"hello\\\") }\")\n\t\tsession.CreateOpenCodeTranscript(\"Add color output\", []FileChange{\n\t\t\t{Path: \"app.go\", Content: \"package main\\nimport \\\"fmt\\\"\\nfunc main() { fmt.Println(\\\"hello\\\") }\"},\n\t\t})\n\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"turn-end 2 error: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// === Verify: a new checkpoint was created for turn 2 ===\n\t\tpoints2 := env.GetRewindPoints()\n\t\tif len(points2) == 0 {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected rewind point after turn 2 (resumed session), got none\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// The checkpoint should reference app.go as a modified file\n\t\tlastPoint := points2[len(points2)-1]\n\t\tfoundAppGo := false\n\t\tfor _, f := range lastPoint.FilesTouched {\n\t\t\tif f == \"app.go\" {\n\t\t\t\tfoundAppGo = true\n\t\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !foundAppGo {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"turn 2 checkpoint should include app.go in FilesTouched, got: %v\", lastPoint.FilesTouched)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// For manual-commit: commit turn 2 and verify second condensation\n\t\tif strategyName == strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit {\n\t\t\tenv.GitCommitWithShadowHooks(\"Add color output\", \"app.go\")\n\n\t\t\tcheckpointID2 := env.TryGetLatestCheckpointID()\n\t\t\tif checkpointID2 == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected second checkpoint on metadata branch after turn 2 commit\")\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif checkpointID2 == checkpointID {\n\t\t\t\tt.Error(\"second checkpoint ID should differ from first\")\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}"
}

   278→// TestOpenCodeResumedSessionAfterCommit verifies that resuming an OpenCode session
   279→// after a commit correctly creates a checkpoint for the second turn.
   280→//
   281→// This tests the scenario:
   282→//  1. Turn 1: create new file → checkpoint → user commits (condensation)
   283→//  2. Turn 2 (resumed): modify the now-tracked file → checkpoint should be created
   284→//
   285→// Bug: transcript-based file extraction used "file_path" key but OpenCode exports use
   286→// "filePath" (camelCase). Turn 1 worked because the file was NEW (detected via git status).
   287→// Turn 2 failed because the file was now tracked+modified — only detectable via transcript
   288→// extraction, which silently returned empty due to the key mismatch.
   289→func TestOpenCodeResumedSessionAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {
   290→	t.Parallel()
   291→
   292→	RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
   293→		env.InitEntireWithAgent(strategyName, agent.AgentNameOpenCode)
   294→
   295→		session := env.NewOpenCodeSession()
   296→		transcriptPath := session.TranscriptPath
   297→
   298→		// === Turn 1: Create a new file ===
   299→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeSessionStart(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {
   300→			t.Fatalf("session-start error: %v", err)
   301→		}
   302→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, "Create app.go"); err != nil {
   303→			t.Fatalf("turn-start 1 error: %v", err)
   304→		}
   305→
   306→		env.WriteFile("app.go", "package main\nfunc main() {}")
   307→		session.CreateOpenCodeTranscript("Create app.go", []FileChange{
   308→			{Path: "app.go", Content: "package main\nfunc main() {}"},
   309→		})
   310→
   311→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {
   312→			t.Fatalf("turn-end 1 error: %v", err)
   313→		}
   314→
   315→		points1 := env.GetRewindPoints()
   316→		if len(points1) == 0 {
   317→			t.Fatal("expected rewind point after turn 1")
   318→		}
   319→
   320→		// === User commits (triggers condensation, deletes shadow branch) ===
   321→		env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Create app", "app.go")
   322→
   323→		// Verify condensation happened
   324→		checkpointID := env.TryGetLatestCheckpointID()
   325→		if checkpointID == "" {
   326→			t.Fatal("expected checkpoint on metadata branch after commit")
   327→		}
   328→
   329→		// === Turn 2 (resumed): Modify the now-tracked file ===
   330→		// The file is already committed — it will show as "modified" in git status,
   331→		// NOT as "new". This means it must be detected via transcript extraction.
   332→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnStart(session.ID, transcriptPath, "Add color output"); err != nil {
   333→			t.Fatalf("turn-start 2 error: %v", err)
   334→		}
   335→
   336→		env.WriteFile("app.go", "package main\nimport \"fmt\"\nfunc main() { fmt.Println(\"hello\") }")
   337→		session.CreateOpenCodeTranscript("Add color output", []FileChange{
   338→			{Path: "app.go", Content: "package main\nimport \"fmt\"\nfunc main() { fmt.Println(\"hello\") }"},
   339→		})
   340→
   341→		if err := env.SimulateOpenCodeTurnEnd(session.ID, transcriptPath); err != nil {
   342→			t.Fatalf("turn-end 2 error: %v", err)
   343→		}
   344→
   345→		// === Verify: a new checkpoint was created for turn 2 ===
   346→		points2 := env.GetRewindPoints()
   347→		if len(points2) == 0 {
   348→			t.Fatal("expected rewind point after turn 2 (resumed session), got none")
   349→		}
   350→
   351→		// The checkpoint should reference app.go as a modified file
   352→		lastPoint := points2[len(points2)-1]
   353→		foundAppGo := false
   354→		for _, f := range lastPoint.FilesTouched {
   355→			if f == "app.go" {
   356→				foundAppGo = true
   357→				break
   358→			}
   359→		}
   360→		if !foundAppGo {
   361→			t.Errorf("turn 2 checkpoint should include app.go in FilesTouched, got: %v", lastPoint.FilesTouched)
   362→		}
   363→
   364→		// For manual-commit: commit turn 2 and verify second condensation
   365→		if strategyName == strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit {
   366→			env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add color output", "app.go")
   367→
   368→			checkpointID2 := env.TryGetLatestCheckpointID()
   369→			if checkpointID2 == "" {
   370→				t.Fatal("expected second checkpoint on metadata branch after turn 2 commit")
   371→			}
   372→			if checkpointID2 == checkpointID {
   373→				t.Error("second checkpoint ID should differ from first")
   374→			}
   375→		}
   376→	})
   377→}
   378→
```

Let me fix both. First, the summarize test issue — I need to check how the summarize code extracts file paths.

   227→// extractGenericToolDetail extracts an appropriate detail string from a tool's input/args map.

   229→func extractGenericToolDetail(input map[string]interface{}) string {
   230→	for _, key := range []string{"description", "command", "file_path", "path", "pattern"} {

   232→			return v
   233→		}
   234→	}
   235→	return ""

   279→// after a commit correctly creates a checkpoint for the second turn.
   280→//
   281→// This tests the scenario:
   282→//  1. Turn 1: create new file → checkpoint → user commits (condensation)

   284→//
   285→// Bug: transcript-based file extraction used "file_path" key but OpenCode exports use
   286→// "filePath" (camelCase). Turn 1 worked because the file was NEW (detected via git status).
   287→// Turn 2 failed because the file was now tracked+modified — only detectable via transcript
   288→// extraction, which silently returned empty due to the key mismatch.

   210→		}

> AGENT

You're right to flag this — `extractGenericToolDetail` is shared between Gemini (line 163) and OpenCode (line 206). Adding `"filePath"` to the list doesn't break Gemini — it just adds an additional key to check. The function tries keys in order and returns the first match. Gemini uses `"file_path"` which is still in the list. The only change is that OpenCode's `"filePath"` will now match before `"file_path"` does, which is correct because we want camelCase to match for OpenCode. For Gemini, `"filePath"` won't exist in the input so it falls through to the next keys as before.

Let me verify Gemini's tool input format to be sure.

## Summary: Transcript File Naming and Format Across Agents

Based on my exploration of the codebase, here are the key findings:

### 1. **Transcript Filename is ALWAYS `full.jsonl`** 
**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go:28`

```go
const TranscriptFileName = "full.jsonl"
```

This is a hardcoded constant used universally across all agents. The name is misleading for OpenCode, which actually uses JSON (not JSONL).

### 2. **OpenCode Uses JSON Format, Not JSONL**

**Evidence:**
- **OpenCode's transcript format:** Single JSON object `{"info": {...}, "messages": [...]}`
  - Location: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/opencode.go:65-147`
  - Test data shows: `ExportSession{Info: SessionInfo{...}, Messages: []ExportMessage{...}}`
  
- **Claude Code uses JSONL:** One JSON object per line, each line is a complete transcript event
  - Location: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/transcript/parse.go:15-46`
  - Parses with: `json.Unmarshal(lineBytes, &line)` for each line

- **Gemini CLI uses JSON:** Single JSON object like OpenCode

### 3. **Format-Aware Chunking Handles Both Formats**

**Chunking architecture:**
- Location: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/agent/chunking.go:19-66`

```go
// ChunkTranscript - delegates to agent-specific implementation
func ChunkTranscript(content []byte, agentType AgentType) ([][]byte, error)

// ReassembleTranscript - delegates to agent-specific implementation
func ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte, agentType AgentType) ([]byte, error)
```

**Agent-specific chunking:**
- **Claude Code:** Chunks at JSONL line boundaries (`ChunkJSONL`)
- **OpenCode:** Chunks by splitting message array (`ExportSession.Messages`)
- **Gemini CLI:** Similar to OpenCode - JSON message array splitting

### 4. **How Transcripts Are Read Back**

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/committed.go:1489-1567` (`readTranscriptFromTree`)

```go
// Detects chunk files (.001, .002, etc.) and reassembles
// Uses agent type to call correct ReassembleTranscript method
chunks, _ := agent.ReassembleTranscript(chunks, agentType)
```

**Flow:**
1. Reads transcript files from git tree (can be chunked or non-chunked)
2. Passes `agentType` to `ReassembleTranscript()`
3. Agent-specific reassembly combines chunks back together
4. Falls back to JSONL if agent type is unknown/not found

### 5. **Subagent Transcripts Use Hardcoded JSONL Naming**

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/transcript.go:276-281`

```go
func AgentTranscriptPath(transcriptDir, agentID string) string {
	return filepath.Join(transcriptDir, fmt.Sprintf("agent-%s.jsonl", agentID))
}
```

Subagent transcripts are ALWAYS named `agent-{agentId}.jsonl` (JSONL format), regardless of parent agent type.

### 6. **No Agent-Specific Transcript File Naming at Session Level**

The main transcript is always `full.jsonl` regardless of agent. There is NO mapping like:
- ❌ `full.jsonl` for Claude Code
- ❌ `full.json` for OpenCode
- ❌ `full-opencode.json` for OpenCode

Instead, the agent type is determined separately:
- **From metadata:** `CommittedMetadata.Agent` field stores the agent type
- **From events:** Agent informs during checkpoint saves via `StepContext.AgentType`

### 7. **Format Detection for Chunking**

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/agent/chunking.go:165-187`

```go
func DetectAgentTypeFromContent(content []byte) AgentType {
	// Detects Gemini JSON format (starts with {, has messages array)
	// Returns AgentTypeGemini or empty string (defaults to JSONL)
}
```

When agent type is unknown, detection tries Gemini JSON format; otherwise defaults to JSONL.

### 8. **How Claude Code's Transcript Position Works**

**Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/transcript.go:368-417`

For Claude Code (JSONL):
- "Position" = line number (0-indexed)
- Line count tracks total lines in transcript
- `ParseFromFileAtLine(path, startLine)` reads from specific line onwards

For OpenCode (JSON):
- "Position" = message count (0-indexed)
- **Location:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/transcript.go` (would need to check)
- Different position semantics but same interface

### Key Insight: No Transcript Format Compatibility Issues

The existing code **already handles JSON content in `full.jsonl` files correctly**:

1. **Writing:** Agent passes any content via `ReadTranscript()`, no parsing
2. **Reading:** Uses agent type to call correct `ReassembleTranscript()` method
3. **Fallback:** If agent type unknown, tries format detection or defaults to JSONL
4. **Redaction:** `redact.JSONLBytes()` is tried first; if it fails, falls back to plain string redaction:
   - Location: `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/committed.go:1438-1446`

```go
if strings.HasSuffix(treePath, ".jsonl") {
    redacted, jsonlErr := redact.JSONLBytes(content)
    if jsonlErr != nil {
        redacted = redact.Bytes(content)  // Fallback to plain redaction
    }
    content = redacted
}
```

### Recommendations

The architecture is **format-agnostic at the I/O level**:
- Transcript filename (`full.jsonl`) is just a label
- Actual format is handled by agent-specific `ChunkTranscript()`/`ReassembleTranscript()` implementations
- Agent type drives format interpretation when reading
- The system gracefully handles non-JSONL content in `.jsonl` files

No changes needed for OpenCode JSON support - it already works correctly through agent type routing.

## Research Findings: `extractGenericToolDetail` Usage and Agent Format Mixing

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's what I found:

### 1. **Usage of `extractGenericToolDetail` in summarize.go**

The function `extractGenericToolDetail` at line 229 of `cmd/entire/cli/summarize/summarize.go` is called in **two distinct code paths**:

1. **Gemini path (Line 163)**: `buildCondensedTranscriptFromGemini()` 
   - Called when processing Gemini CLI transcripts (JSON format)
   - Receives `tc.Args` which is `map[string]interface{}`
   - Tool calls: `toolCall.Args["file_path"]`, `toolCall.Args["path"]`, `toolCall.Args["filename"]`

2. **OpenCode path (Line 206)**: `buildCondensedTranscriptFromOpenCode()`
   - Called when processing OpenCode export transcripts (JSON format)
   - Receives `part.State.Input` which is `map[string]interface{}`
   - Tool calls: `part.State.Input["filePath"]` (camelCase - native OpenCode format)

### 2. **Tool Input Key Naming Differences**

**Gemini CLI tools** (from `cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/transcript.go:138-144`):
- Check order: `"file_path"` → `"path"` → `"filename"`
- Snake_case convention
- Example from test: `{"file_path":"/src/main.go"}`

**OpenCode tools** (from `cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/transcript.go:164-173`):
- Check order: `"filePath"` → `"file_path"` → `"path"` → `"file"` → `"filename"`
- CamelCase native (`"filePath"`) listed first
- Snake_case fallbacks included
- Example from test: `{"filePath": "main.go"}`

**The current `extractGenericToolDetail` check order (line 230)**:
```go
for _, key := range []string{"description", "command", "filePath", "file_path", "path", "pattern"}
```

### 3. **Key Findings**

**Problem Identified**: The function handles BOTH camelCase (`filePath`) and snake_case (`file_path`) keys in a single generic function, which works but obscures the architectural difference:
- OpenCode's native format uses camelCase (`filePath`)
- Gemini's format uses snake_case (`file_path`)

**Evidence from tests**:
- `summarize_test.go:740` - OpenCode test uses `"filePath"`
- `summarize_test.go:464` - Gemini test uses `"file_path"`
- `opencode/transcript_test.go:190` - Test comment confirms: "camelCase filePath (OpenCode native format)"
- `opencode/transcript_test.go:191` - Test comment for snake_case: "snake_case file_path"

### 4. **Specialized Functions vs Generic Function**

There are **already agent-specific extraction functions**:

1. **`extractFilePathFromInput()` in `cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode/transcript.go:164`**
   - Specialized for OpenCode
   - Checks: `["filePath", "file_path", "path", "file", "filename"]` (camelCase first)
   - Used by `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset()` and `ExtractModifiedFiles()`

2. **No equivalent in `geminicli/transcript.go`**
   - Gemini file extraction is inline in `ExtractModifiedFilesFromTranscript()` (lines 138-144)
   - Checks: `"file_path"` → `"path"` → `"filename"` (snake_case only)
   - Does NOT check for `"filePath"`

3. **The generic function in summarize.go**
   - Used by BOTH agents' condensation paths
   - Attempts to be agent-agnostic by checking both formats
   - Makes the dependency implicit rather than explicit

### 5. **Other Format Mixing in Codebase**

Searched across the codebase and found NO other instances of similar format mixing. The file path extraction is unique to tools like "Read", "Write", "Edit" where agents have different conventions.

### 6. **Tests Confirming Current Behavior**

- `summarize_test.go:498-530` - `TestBuildCondensedTranscriptFromBytes_GeminiToolCallArgShapes()` tests various keys including `"path"`, `"description"`, `"pattern"`
- `summarize_test.go:740` - OpenCode test shows `"filePath"` in tool input
- `opencode/transcript_test.go:182-209` - Comprehensive test of `extractFilePathFromInput()` showing all supported keys

### Summary of Architecture

| Aspect | OpenCode | Gemini | Current Generic Function |
|--------|----------|--------|--------------------------|
| Native key format | camelCase (`filePath`) | snake_case (`file_path`) | Handles both |
| File extraction function | `extractFilePathFromInput()` (specific) | Inline logic in transcript.go | `extractGenericToolDetail()` (generic) |
| Used by summarize.go | Yes, via generic function | Yes, via generic function | Both |
| Ordering preference | camelCase first | snake_case only | Mixed (camelCase 3rd) |

**Conclusion**: There IS format mixing between agents in the summarize tool, and while the current generic function works, it could be separated for clarity. The current approach treats tool detail extraction as agent-agnostic, but in reality, OpenCode and Gemini have fundamentally different key naming conventions.

Important discovery: `redact.JSONLContent` processes each line individually. For a compact (single-line) JSON object, this would work fine since the whole object is one line. For a pretty-printed JSON object, each line would be parsed individually -- most lines wouldn't be valid JSON, so they'd fall through to `String(line)` (plain string redaction at line 148). This actually works but is suboptimal. However, this is a pre-existing issue for Gemini too, and it's not part of the filename fix. The `JSONLBytes` call in `writeTranscript` is a safety-net redaction and the fallback to `String(line)` for non-JSON lines still catches secrets.

So the `redact.JSONLBytes` call in `writeTranscript` does NOT need changing for this fix. It will work (with degraded redaction quality) for JSON transcripts. If we wanted to improve it, that's a separate issue.

Here is the comprehensive implementation plan for both issues.

## Issue 1: Unmixing Agent Formats in `extractGenericToolDetail`

### Problem
`extractGenericToolDetail()` in `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/summarize.go:229` is shared between OpenCode (camelCase `"filePath"`) and Gemini (snake_case `"file_path"`). Adding `"filePath"` to the shared function mixes format concerns.

### Solution
Revert the shared function to Gemini-only keys, and create an OpenCode-specific function.

### Changes

**Single file: `cmd/entire/cli/summarize/summarize.go`**

1. At line 229, revert `extractGenericToolDetail` to remove `"filePath"`:
```go
func extractGenericToolDetail(input map[string]interface{}) string {
    for _, key := range []string{"description", "command", "file_path", "path", "pattern"} {
        if v, ok := input[key].(string); ok && v != "" {
            return v
        }
    }
    return ""
}
```

2. Add a new OpenCode-specific function (near line 227, before or after the generic one):
```go
// extractOpenCodeToolDetail extracts a detail string from an OpenCode tool's input map.
// OpenCode uses camelCase keys (e.g., "filePath" not "file_path").
func extractOpenCodeToolDetail(input map[string]interface{}) string {
    for _, key := range []string{"description", "command", "filePath", "path", "pattern"} {
        if v, ok := input[key].(string); ok && v != "" {
            return v
        }
    }
    return ""
}
```

3. At line 206, change `buildCondensedTranscriptFromOpenCode` to use the new function:
```go
ToolDetail: extractOpenCodeToolDetail(part.State.Input),
```

---

## Issue 2: OpenCode Transcript Saved as `.jsonl` but Is Actually JSON

### Problem
All agents use `paths.TranscriptFileName = "full.jsonl"`. OpenCode (and Gemini) transcripts are JSON, not JSONL. Saving them as `full.jsonl` is misleading.

### Design Principles
- Add a new `TranscriptFileNameJSON = "full.json"` constant.
- New writes for JSON-format agents use `full.json`.
- All read paths try `full.jsonl`, `full.json`, and `full.log` in order, so old checkpoints remain readable.
- The resolution function lives in `agent` package (not `paths`) to avoid import cycles.

### Changes

#### File 1: `cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go`

Add new constant at line 29 (between `TranscriptFileName` and `TranscriptFileNameLegacy`):

```go
TranscriptFileName       = "full.jsonl"
TranscriptFileNameJSON   = "full.json"       // NEW
TranscriptFileNameLegacy = "full.log"
```

Also add a package-level variable listing all transcript filenames in preference order (useful for read paths):

```go
// TranscriptFileNames lists all recognized transcript filenames in preference order.
// Used by read paths to try each filename when the agent type is unknown.
var TranscriptFileNames = []string{TranscriptFileName, TranscriptFileNameJSON, TranscriptFileNameLegacy}
```

#### File 2: `cmd/entire/cli/agent/registry.go` (or new file `agent/transcript_filename.go`)

Add a function to resolve the correct filename:

```go
// TranscriptFileNameForType returns the appropriate transcript filename for the given agent type.
// JSON-format agents (OpenCode, Gemini) use "full.json"; JSONL agents (Claude) use "full.jsonl".
func TranscriptFileNameForType(agentType AgentType) string {
    switch agentType {
    case AgentTypeOpenCode, AgentTypeGemini:
        return paths.TranscriptFileNameJSON
    default:
        return paths.TranscriptFileName
    }
}
```

#### File 3: `cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` (WRITE path)

At line 203, replace the hardcoded filename:

```go
// Current:
logFile := filepath.Join(sessionDirAbs, paths.TranscriptFileName)
// ...
fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Copied transcript to: %s\n", sessionDir+"/"+paths.TranscriptFileName)

// New:
transcriptFilename := agent.TranscriptFileNameForType(ag.Type())
logFile := filepath.Join(sessionDirAbs, transcriptFilename)
// ...
fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Copied transcript to: %s\n", sessionDir+"/"+transcriptFilename)
```

#### File 4: `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/committed.go` (WRITE + READ paths)

**4a. `writeTranscript` (line 534)** - Use agent-appropriate filename for chunk names:

Add at beginning of function:
```go
transcriptBase := agent.TranscriptFileNameForType(opts.Agent)
```
Then replace all `paths.TranscriptFileName` references in this function with `transcriptBase`.

**4b. `writeSessionToSubdirectory` (line 310)** - Use agent-appropriate filename:
```go
transcriptBase := agent.TranscriptFileNameForType(opts.Agent)
filePaths.Transcript = "/" + sessionPath + transcriptBase
```

**4c. `replaceTranscript` (line 1219)** - Clean up old format AND write new format:
```go
func (s *GitStore) replaceTranscript(transcript []byte, agentType agent.AgentType, sessionPath string, entries map[string]object.TreeEntry) error {
    transcriptBase := agent.TranscriptFileNameForType(agentType)
    
    // Remove existing transcript files of ALL formats (handles format migration)
    for _, base := range []string{paths.TranscriptFileName, paths.TranscriptFileNameJSON} {
        fullBase := sessionPath + base
        for key := range entries {
            if key == fullBase || strings.HasPrefix(key, fullBase+".") {
                delete(entries, key)
            }
        }
    }
    
    // Write chunks with new base name
    chunks, err := agent.ChunkTranscript(transcript, agentType)
    // ... use transcriptBase for ChunkFileName ...
}
```

**4d. `readTranscriptFromTree` (line 1492)** - Try all filenames:

Refactor to iterate over all transcript base names. The key change: instead of only looking for `paths.TranscriptFileName` and chunks, also look for `paths.TranscriptFileNameJSON` and its chunks.

```go
func readTranscriptFromTree(tree *object.Tree, agentType agent.AgentType) ([]byte, error) {
    // Try primary formats (JSONL, then JSON) with chunk support
    for _, baseName := range []string{paths.TranscriptFileName, paths.TranscriptFileNameJSON} {
        if result := tryReadTranscriptBase(tree, baseName, agentType); result != nil {
            return result, nil
        }
    }
    
    // Try legacy filename (no chunk support)
    if file, err := tree.File(paths.TranscriptFileNameLegacy); err == nil {
        if content, err := file.Contents(); err == nil {
            return []byte(content), nil
        }
    }
    
    return nil, nil
}

#### File 9: `cmd/entire/cli/rewind.go` (READ paths, lines 295, 496)

These are local-file references used as last-resort fallbacks. Currently they use `TranscriptFileNameLegacy`. Since the write path will now write `full.json` for OpenCode, the rewind local-file fallback should also try the new filename. However, the primary restore paths (lines 303-317) go through strategy/checkpoint methods that will be updated. The local-file fallback (line 320-326) only triggers when both strategy and shadow methods fail.

For completeness, update the fallback to try multiple filenames:
```go
// Try to find local transcript file
var transcriptFile string
for _, name := range paths.TranscriptFileNames {
    candidate := filepath.Join(selectedPoint.MetadataDir, name)
    if _, err := os.Stat(candidate); err == nil {
        transcriptFile = candidate
        break
    }
}
if transcriptFile == "" {
    transcriptFile = filepath.Join(selectedPoint.MetadataDir, paths.TranscriptFileNameLegacy)
}
```

### Backward Compatibility

- Old checkpoints written as `full.jsonl` continue to work because all read paths try `full.jsonl` first.
- New OpenCode/Gemini checkpoints are written as `full.json`.
- The `TranscriptFileNames` slice ensures consistent fallback ordering across all read sites.
- Chunked transcripts also work: chunks follow the base name (`full.json.001` for JSON agents, `full.jsonl.001` for JSONL agents).
- The `replaceTranscript` method cleans up both formats when replacing, so format migration on update is handled.

- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go` - Add `TranscriptFileNameJSON` constant and `TranscriptFileNames` list
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli_experiments/cmd/entire/cli/agent/registry.go` - Add `TranscriptFileNameForType()` function
